## Addendum 209 (10:41, Nov 10; built, in review, not yet run): the online teachers on contest coding (user: "can we try Dx4 on contest coding? That seems like the most imporant next tests we run", "before anything else", "thats really the root of why we are doing this... agentic coding")

Why: the paper's coding study (174-190) never ran setup D. Its corrector routes were an anchor, an offline on-policy teacher (opd_c, opd_cf; the cross-model opd_b was never run) and a distilled LoRA, with correctors fitted the earlier way (a size term, no student samples, 600 steps); its sentence "the corrector stored nothing more" does not cover the telepathy-like teacher of 206. This addendum runs 206's teachers on the coding study's problems and scores them with the coding study's own evaluation (problems solved by hidden tests), so that the method is judged on task success and not only on closeness to E4B.

Design. One teacher and one seed per Colab G4 session (colab/xq_lcb.sh, launcher dreamer/xq_lcb_launch.sh; code9 = 206's code7 with an lcb stream in online.py's train_online, every other line of that function unchanged; transfer/lcb_rows.py builds the rows; xlearn.py's evaluate only accepts the new arm names). Student Gemma 4 E2B, teacher Gemma 4 E4B, the 4090's dequantized weights and the correctors of 206, sha256-checked.
- Stream: the coding study's 299 row problems (older LiveCodeBench; each has E4B's own passing solution, here the shortest, xl_rows_short.jsonl); 598 items = two seeded passes (the coding study's dose of two epochs and, within 2 items, 206's stream length). An item is the problem prompt as the evaluation sends it; the student samples two continuations of at most 512 tokens (xlearn's on-policy length; 206 used 256), each ending at its own closing fence.
- Teachers: D4 (base E2B corrected by 199's 4x cross-model corrector, fed E4B's states on the sampled text; arm onl_d4); C (E4B's own distribution on the sampled text; onl_c); copying (cross-entropy on E4B's verified solution, no sampling: online SFT; onl_sft); S4 (the 4x self-corrector; onl_s4). All with 206's recipe: rank-16 LoRA, lr 3e-4, keep 1.0 (KL to the original E2B on one untaught prose, code or story row per item), replay every 4th item.
- The correctors are 206's own: fitted on agent replies, prose, code chunks, questions, stories and E2B's samples, never on contest problems or their solutions. That is deliberate (the same teacher as the paper's best student) and is the test's main limit: a corrector fitted on contest solutions is a different experiment (181's refit).
- Order: seed 1 of D4, C and copying together; seed 2 of the three; then S4 at seeds 1 and 2 (no session starts after 02:00 on Nov 11). About 5 h and 50 CU per session (balance 687 CU).
- Measures, from the coding study's evaluation in the same session (E2B alone re-answered there, greedy, 768-token cap, hidden tests): held solved (of the 188 newer problems; E2B alone solved 57 in the coding study, E4B's greedy answer 74); fixed (row problems E2B alone fails, now solved; 151 in the coding study); lost (solved alone, now failed; row and held problems); the cross-entropy of E4B's passing held solutions; the off-task rows. From the stream: held cross-entropy to E4B on 60 held coding rows every 50 items, prequential gain, and at item 0 the teacher's own held cross-entropy (the corrected base E2B). And 206's 240-point agent gate on the coding-taught LoRA (fixed and broken agent decisions). The copying session of each seed also re-evaluates the coding study's offline SFT adapter of that seed (rank 64, 186) as a reference on this base.
- Comparisons, pooled over the seeds run, exact sign tests on discordant problems as in the coding study: D4 against E2B alone, against C, against copying; C against copying; D4 against S4 when S4 has run.

Rules, fixed now. The primary measure is held solved; fixed is reported beside it with the note that copying trains on those problems' own answers. (1) "Telepathy-like teaching carries to contest coding" if D4 solves more held problems than E2B alone in each seed and the pooled sign test against E2B alone has p < 0.05. (2) "It beats copying there" only if D4 solves more held problems than both C and copying in each seed and both pooled tests have p < 0.05. (3) "It matches copying" if no pooled test between D4 and either copying arm reaches p < 0.05 and the pooled held difference is within 10 problems per seed (the coding study's noise). (4) "Copying is better" if either copying arm beats D4 at p < 0.05. (5) The lost counts decide the forgetting sentence: fewer lost under D4 than under copying in each seed. One seed alone supports no ranking (two SFT retrainings differed by 6 held solves). If only seed 1 is complete at the paper's freeze, the paper reports it as one seed with no claim. The paper's coding sentence is rewritten to whatever these rules give.

Expectations and power. The room is small: E4B's greedy answer solves 74 of the 188 held problems and E2B alone 57, and the coding study's SFT brought E2B to 68; a taught arm can be expected between 57 and about 72, and two seeds separate arms only when they differ by more than about 10 per seed. So the likeliest reading is rule 3 or no claim.

Predictions:
1. The teacher (base E2B corrected by the 4x cross-model corrector) is closer to E4B on the held coding rows than E2B alone at item 0 (80%); by at least a tenth of E2B alone's cross-entropy (50%).
2. D4's student ends closer to E4B on the held coding rows than C's in both seeds (65%), and is at C's final level by item 150 in both (50%).
3. D4 solves more held problems than E2B alone in both seeds (55%); the pooled test has p < 0.05 (30%).
4. Copying (online SFT) solves more held problems than E2B alone in both seeds (65%); p < 0.05 pooled (40%).
5. Rule 2 (D4 beats both copying arms) is met (8%); rule 4 (a copying arm beats D4) is met (20%); rule 3 or no claim otherwise.
6. Copying fixes more row problems than D4 in both seeds (85%), by more than 30 per seed (60%).
7. D4 loses fewer solved problems (row and held) than copying in both seeds (55%).
8. On the agent gate the coding-taught D4 LoRA breaks at most 6 of the 168 agreeing decisions in both seeds (65%).
9. S4's teacher (the self-corrector) is less close to E4B on the held coding rows at item 0 than D4's (75%): off its fitting text the self-corrector has less to give.
Limits: one model pair; the correctors were not fitted on contest coding; two seeds at most for most arms; solves near 60-70 of 188 with run-to-run noise near 6; the stream's second pass revisits every problem (the prequential gain of the second half is not a first-visit measure); samples longer than 512 tokens are cut.

**209, review and start (10:53, Nov 10).** A subagent review of online.py's lcb stream, lcb_rows.py, the job script and the launcher found no blocker. It checked on all 299 rows that the rows tokenize as the builder asserts (BOS first, no padding or BOS inside, the solution's separate tokenization equal to the joint one, exact decode-back, every solution ending in one fence token) and that the fence stop does not fire on the prompt's own opening fence. Applied: the copying session loads the stored rank-64 SFT reference adapter in its small evaluation too and stops if it is missing; the launcher compares its frozen copy with the tree; its cap list also names 206d's sessions (206d's launcher must list the xqc sessions before it is started again). Kept as pre-registered, with their cost stated: samples of at most 512 tokens, the coding study's on-policy length (54 of the 299 row solutions are longer, up to 762 tokens, and the evaluation answers up to 768; D4, S4 and C share the cap, copying sees whole solutions, so the comparison of copying with the sampled arms is tilted toward copying on long solutions); the order (the two copying arms at both seeds before S4). The stored SFT adapter is a reference, not an arm: trained offline at rank 64 on E2B dequantized on Colab, evaluated here on the 4090's dequantized E2B. The launcher starts now; xqgC3 (206) still holds one of the three slots until about 11:30.

The answer repeats the starter signature, writes "# ... implementation ..." and closes the fence: 24 of the 188 held answers and 43 of the 299 row answers, all on LeetCode-format problems. E2B alone: 11 and 15 (other wordings of the same placeholder). C and copying: 0 and 0 at seed 2 (C 1 held at seed 1). The unfitted D4 at seed 1: 1 and 1.

On the other 164 held problems: the fitted D4 61, C 67, copying 64, alone 53; on the other 256 row problems: 182, 188, 204, 132.

It explains most of the trained problems the fitted D4 breaks, and little of the held gap (2 to 5 of the 8 problems). Whether it comes from the fit, the seed or the kind of teacher is not known; seed 1 of the fitted D4 shows whether it repeats.

**Amendment 209d (22:10, Nov 10; a diagnostic; built, NOT RUN, see the entry below): who prefers the placeholder stub.** User, after the analysis above: "can you research why its doing this? Is it maybe overfitting on the corrector?"; "because I think if we can fix it, it might actually solve more".
- What the code says: the teaching loop of C, D4 and S4 is on-policy (online.py train_online). At each item the student samples two answers of its own at the problem's prompt (T 1.0, at most 512 tokens, to the closing fence) and is pulled, token by token along its own text, toward the teacher's distribution there (KL from the student to the teacher). E4B's verified solution is never the text; only copying trains on it. So a student ends with the stub only if its teacher's distribution, on the student's own text, supports the stub.
- The probe (transfer/stub_probe.py, colab/xq_stubprobe.sh; one G4 session; forward passes and samples, no training): on the 235 LeetCode-format problems (147 row, 88 held) the text is the answer up to the first signature, then "# ... implementation ...", then the closing fence (on the 67 problems where the fitted D4's student of seed 2 wrote the stub, its own text). Read: the log-probability of each token of the stub's body under E2B alone, E4B, base E2B corrected by the cross-model corrector fitted with the contest rows, base E2B corrected by the unfitted 4x one, and the students (the fitted D4 at seed 2, the unfitted D4 at seed 1, C at seed 2, copying at seed 2; the fitted D4 at seed 1 if its adapter is ready). And 96 on-policy samples (48 row prompts, two each, drawn as the teaching loop draws them) from E2B alone and from each student, with the share of stubs.
- Readings, fixed now: (a) "the fitted corrector teaches the stub" if the fitted teacher would write the stub itself (every body token is its most likely one) on clearly more problems than E2B alone and than the unfitted teacher, while E4B would on few. Then the fault is in the corrector's fit (a candidate: E2B's own stub samples among the fit's on-policy rows, where E4B's distribution along a stub already begun completes it). (b) "the on-policy loop does it" if the fitted teacher supports the stub no more than E2B alone and the student still writes it. (c) If E4B itself supports the stub, that is reported, and the question becomes why copying its distribution removes it.
- Predictions: 1. The fitted teacher gives the stub's body a higher probability than E2B alone on most of the 67 problems (70%) and would write it itself on at least a third of them (50%). 2. E4B would write the stub itself on under 5% of the 235 problems (75%). 3. The unfitted 4x teacher would write it on fewer problems than the fitted teacher (70%). 4. Stubs are a larger share of the fitted D4 student's samples than of E2B alone's (85%); E2B alone's samples hold at least 5% stubs (60%).
- No rule of 209 or 209a depends on this. A fix, if the cause is clear, is a new amendment with its own rule.

Look at that data and see if it has that stub in it... we also need to make sure that we train it on actual good tasks that succeed." - Target on every draw: E4B's next-token distribution along the drawn text (and E2B's own where both models pick the same token).
- lcb, 1,448 draws: the 299 row problems with E4B's shortest verified solution. All 299 pass the tests and end with the fence; none is a stub; 3 hold a "# ..." comment.
- onpolicy_lcb, 917 draws: E2B's own sample at a row problem's prompt, drawn inside the fit at temperature 1.0 with no top-p, at most 512 tokens, to its closing fence. Not stored, not tested, not filtered. A proxy for their quality: E2B alone's greedy answers to the same 299 problems are 148 passing, 15 stubs, 106 unfinished within 768 tokens, 30 other failures; samples at temperature 1.0 are not better.
- agent, 942 draws: E4B's replies at decision points of its own failed runs (dreamer/s2_calls.py: tasks the base 31B resolved and base E4B did not). (omitted for brevity)": E4B eliding code it quotes), 5 the exact words "... implementation", 5 a def whose body is only comments. onpolicy, 491 draws: E2B's own samples at those points, untested.

- So only the lcb part (30% of the draws) is text of a task that succeeded. In the fit session (transfer/lcb_own.py rows) every row is run on its tests again and written only if it passes there.
**The smoke test (lcb_own.py check), before any fit.** Every fit row and every row of the teaching stream is read back from the tokens the fit trains on and must decode to its prompt, end with its fence, pass every test of its problem in that session (a failure is tried once more alone), parse, be no stub, hold no placeholder comment (fit rows), fit the fit's lengths without a cut, belong to a row problem, and repeat no other row; the fit's weights may name only fit_lcb, prose, question and story. Unless it prints DATA_CHECK_OK the session stops and nothing is fitted.
**The fit.** 199's 4x settings unchanged (2,400 steps, two draws per step, lr 3e-4 with cosine decay, no size term, do-no-harm 0.5, noise 0.1, depth drop 0.15, seed 7), the cross-model corrector from scratch, draw weights fit_lcb 0.7, prose 0.1, question 0.1, story 0.1: about 3,360 draws on the verified solutions (3.6 each) and 1,440 on general text. No agent rows (E4B's failed runs), no on-policy samples (untested), no source-file code rows. Stated: the calibration rows, which only set the spread of the states, are still 48 rows of the 1x pool (agent and mixed rows).
**Teaching (online.py, ON_VERIFY=1).** As 209, except that each of the student's two samples per item is run on the problem's tests: the student learns on the samples that pass (and end with their fence), with the same teacher and loss; when none passes, on E4B's verified solution of that problem (the row's own text), the same teacher and loss (not for the 3 row problems whose solution holds a placeholder comment: there only the keep term moves the student). Arms, seeds 1 and 2: onl_d4v (D4 with the corrector fitted here) and onl_cv (C, E4B's own distribution, with the same filter: the filter's control). Evaluation and agent gate as 209. Launcher dreamer/xq_lcbv_launch.sh, job colab/xq_lcbv.sh, code frozen as code10 (on/code9 is not touched); at most 3 sessions; no start after 06:00 on Nov 11.
**Rules, fixed now.** Primary measure: held problems solved (of 188), pooled sign tests over the two seeds, gained, lost and net reported. (1) "The clean fit removes the stub" if D4v's student writes the stub on fewer than 10 of the 487 problems in each seed (E2B alone 26; 209a's fitted D4 67 and 72). (2) "The corrector adds to verified teaching" only if D4v solves more held problems than Cv in each seed and the pooled test has p < 0.05; "it matches" if the pooled test does not reach p < 0.05 and the pooled difference is within 10 per seed; "Cv is better" if Cv beats D4v at p < 0.05. (3) Against 209's copying arms (C 75 and 69, copying 76 and 69): 209's rules 2 to 4, read for D4v. (4) "Verified teaching helps" if Cv solves more held problems than 209's C in each seed with pooled p < 0.05. One seed alone supports no ranking.

D4v solves at least 66 held problems at seed 1 (50%); at least 69 in one seed (35%); rule 2's "adds" (10%), "matches" (60%). 5. At least half of the learned items have a passing sample (55%). 6. Rule 4 is met (15%).

## Amendment 209g (23:53, Nov 10; built and reviewed; the first reading is running, its results unread): a model judge reads every row, the author model rewrites what it flags, the judge reads the rewrite; then both 4x correctors are fitted
User, 23:35 to 23:42: "cant we use deepseek or a different model to review them?"; "I have $15 left... have Deepseek review each of the tasks, if they look bad have the correct model fix it, and then have deepseek review it again"; "once that is done, start the new fit on both the 4x corrector and self-corrector".
**209f did not run.** Its fit session (xqwFit, 23:31) failed in its setup (a token upload) before any job started; it and its launcher were stopped at 23:39 so that the rows go to a second reader first. 209f's word lists are kept only as a comparison in the judge's report.
**The pipeline.**
1. Pool (dreamer/lcb_fit_rows.py pool; runs/xql/own209g/xl_rows_pool.jsonl): every candidate that passed its tests and passes the rules of form (one closing fence, parses, no stub, no placeholder comment), near-copies left out, no cap: 672 rows on 296 problems, 528 by E4B (its shortest verified solution; the build's passing greedy answers and samples) and 144 by E2B (its own greedy answer where it solves the problem alone). The with-a-plan rows stay out (answers to a prompt the fit does not show). Plus 12 calibration rows that are known not to be clean (209e's rows that name a plan; the stub-shaped answer).
2. First reading (dreamer/judge_rows.py): DeepSeek (deepseek/deepseek-v4.1-flash-1017 through OpenRouter; data collection denied; only the problem and the solution are sent) reads each row twice, independently, and answers clean or not_clean with its issues (unfinished, thinking_aloud, refers_to_instructions, leftover_code, hardcoded, other). A row is clean only if both votes say so.
3. Fix (transfer/lcb_fix.py, colab/xq_lcbfix.sh, one G4 session): the author model (E4B for E4B's rows, E2B for E2B's) is given the problem, its own solution and the judge's notes and writes the solution again (a greedy answer and two samples); every rewrite is run on the problem's tests.
4. Second reading: the judge reads the passing rewrites, two votes. A flagged row is replaced by its first rewrite that is clean in both votes (the greedy one first); if none is, the row is left out.
5. Final rows (lcb_fit_rows.py final): at most two per author and problem, near-copies left out. In the fit sessions lcb_own.py runs every row on its tests again and repeats the smoke test (unchanged).
6. Fits and teaching (dreamer/xq_lcbg_launch.sh, colab/xq_lcbv.sh with the self-corrector's cases added; code10 unchanged): the 4x cross-model corrector (xqxFitD) and the 4x self-corrector (xqxFitS), each 2,400 steps with 209f's weights (fit_lcb 0.5, prose 0.17, question 0.17, story 0.16); then, taught only on answers that pass, D4 (xqxD1, xqxD2: onl_d4v), S4 (xqxS1, xqxS2: onl_s4v) and C (xqxC1, xqxC2: onl_cv), in that order, at most 3 sessions, no start after 06:00 on Nov 11.
**Rules.** 209e's four rules, read for these sessions, and 209a's rule 7 read here: "E4B's states matter on coding" if D4v beats S4v on held problems in both seeds with pooled p < 0.05; a tie is reported as on the agent stream. The judge is trusted only if it calls at least 10 of the 12 calibration rows not clean; otherwise its prompt is corrected before any row is decided.
**Predictions.** The judge calls at least 10 of the 12 calibration rows not clean (85%). Between 20% and 45% of the 672 pool rows are not clean in at least one vote (60%). At least half of the flagged rows get a rewrite that passes its tests and both votes (55%). The final file holds at least 500 rows (60%). Both fitted teachers are closer to E4B than E2B alone on the held coding rows (85%); the cross-model one is the closer (75%). Spend on the judge stays under $3 (85%).
**Before the full reading.** The stored OpenRouter key was refused (HTTP 401); the user's key in the repository's .env (git-ignored, never printed) is used; the account showed $15.53. A trial on 10 rows (6 pool rows, 4 calibration rows; 20 calls, $0.006): the 4 calibration rows were called not clean in at least one vote (3 in both), 3 pool rows clean in both votes, 3 flagged in at least one vote for an unused import, a commented-out line of set-up, or a comment that points to the problem's description. One sentence was then added to the judge's instructions (small things such as these are not reasons by themselves); the full reading uses that text. **Review (a subagent).** No fault in the request, the vote logic, the rewrite step or the control path of the job script. Taken: the judge stops at once on an authorisation or payment error; the final step refuses a row with a missing vote instead of reading it as not clean; calibration rows are counted only in their own table; a lost fit session is started again; the filter's control (xqxC1) starts with the two fits, and one seed of every arm runs before the second (order: fits and C1; D1, S1; D2, S2, C2).
**Limits.** The judge is one model with one prompt (written by Claude from the user's words); a rewrite is written under a rewriting instruction and filed under the plain prompt; one fit per corrector; two seeds.

**209g, interim 1 (23:55, Nov 10): the judge's first reading (23:46-23:53; 1,368 calls, $0.30).** Calibration: all 12 rows known not to be clean were flagged, 10 in both votes and 2 in one: the judge passes its bar. Of the 672 pool rows 529 are clean in both votes (E4B 420 of 528, E2B 109 of 144); 143 are flagged (105 in both votes, 38 in one): E4B 108, E2B 35. Issues named over the votes: thinking aloud 170, leftover code 125, refers to instructions 9, unfinished 6, other 6. Examples: a pass left inside an abandoned precomputation, a list built and never used, "Wait, this optimization is incorrect", a comment that says something false about the code. Against 209f's word lists: of the 145 rows the "aloud" list would leave out, the judge calls 49 clean and flags 96; of the 47 "half comments" rows it calls 41 clean; of the 480 rows that pass the lists it flags 41 (what a word list cannot see). **209g, the fix step changed before its results were used (00:11, Nov 11).** User, 00:03: "i thought we had a script that let a model pick up where it left off at a bad turn and complete from there?"; 00:08: "after the resample have deepseek judge them again to verify and have it be fixed if its still not good"; 00:12: "you sanity check the results before we restart the refit if both the 4x correctors".
- The rewrite of step 3 is not used for the fit. It gives the model its solution and an instruction, and the result would be filed under the plain prompt: text written to another prompt, as the with-a-plan rows were, against the rule of the agent rows (10-19: trained text stays the model's own and unconditioned; the tests and the judge only select). The rewrite session did run (xqxFix, 23:54-00:05: 429 rewrites of the 143 rows, 421 passing their tests, 142 rows with a passing fenced rewrite); its output is kept as a comparison and is in no fit row. On the first four rows the rewrites were a third to a half as long as the originals and one had lost every comment.
- Step 3 is now what the agent rows' fork scripts and xlearn.gen_continue do. The judge marks the first bad line of each flagged row (judge_rows.py --locate, two votes, the earlier line is the cut; 286 calls, $0.15: the same line in both votes for 119 of the 143 rows, within two lines for 130; the median row keeps 34% of its lines). The author model is given the plain problem prompt and its own solution up to just before that line, and samples 4 continuations at temperature 0.8 (lcb_fix.py FIX_MODE=continue; a greedy continuation of a greedy answer would write the same text again). Every continuation is run on its tests; the judge reads the passing ones, two votes; the first that is clean in both replaces the row.
- Rounds: a flagged row whose versions pass their tests but are still flagged goes on from its first such version (the judge marks that version's first bad line; lcb_fit_rows.py nextround, contitems); a row with no passing version is sampled again from its last cut. At most two rounds of continuation; a row still without a clean version is left out.
- Before the fits: the final file is checked by hand (counts, the rules of form, rows of both kinds read, the joins at the cuts) and the check is reported to the user.
- Predictions (round 1 unseen): at least 60% of the 143 rows get a continuation that passes its tests (70%); at least 40% get one that is also clean in both votes (55%); after two rounds at least 60% of the 143 are replaced (50%).

**209g, the later rounds follow the agent rows' repair pipeline (00:19, Nov 11; round 1's full results unread).** User, 00:17: "i belive we have a resample script and a judge thst pics the best sample": dreamer/repair.sh, repair_rounds.sh, repair_select.py and think_select.py (10-31), built for agent turns: every state with no accepted sample is sampled again with more samples and more variety (4 at temperature 0.8; then 6 at 1.0; then 8 at 1.0), a round that fixes fewer than 5% of what it tried ends the loop; mechanical checks first, then two judge votes that must both accept; the shortest accepted sample is used; the judges never write. The coding rows now follow it: the tests and the rules of form are the mechanical check; the shortest version that is clean in both votes replaces a flagged row (before: the first one); round 2 gives every row still without an accepted version 3 continuations from its cut and 3 fresh answers to the plain prompt (an empty prefix), at temperature 1.0 (lcb_fit_rows.py nextround; lcb_fix.py FIX_TEMP); round 3, if round 2 fixed at least 5% of what it tried, 4 and 4. The plan of 00:10 to cut a flagged version again at its next bad line is dropped. What was seen before this change: only round 1's small pass (4 rows, 8 continuations, 6 passing their tests): in 3 of the 4 rows both continuations went on thinking aloud as the text before the cut did.

## Amendment 209h (05:48, Nov 11; built and reviewed, not yet run): why the cross-model corrector's student writes the stub, and a fit with verified-token targets
User, 05:33: "i think if we can figure out what is causing the stubs to happen on the cross corector it has tge potental to be the best one".
**What the stored answers show (no new run).**
- The stub is E4B's own. Of E4B's 901 stored greedy answers (the coding study's build: the train pool and the held problems) 75 are the stub, 17 of them on the 188 held problems and 12 on the 299 row problems; 74 hold the words "# ... implementation", the words of all 21 stubs of 209g's D4v student. Of E4B's 1,104 samples 134 are stubs.
- It is tied to problems, not to chance: all 21 stub problems of D4v at seed 1 (11 held, 10 row) are stub problems of 209a's fitted D4 at that seed (27 and 45); 8 and 9 of them also at its seed 2. On them E4B's own greedy answer is a stub 6 times, unfinished 8 times, passing 6 times, wrong once.
- It is not simply E2B's habit left in place: of D4v's 21 stub problems E2B alone writes a stub on 9; D4v no longer writes one on 17 of E2B alone's 26.
- Only the student of the corrector fed E4B's states has it (D4v 21; S4v 0; Cv 0).
- What it costs at seed 1: on the 11 held stub problems nobody solves anything (E2B alone 0, Cv 0, S4v 0, E4B's greedy 0; copying 1); on the 10 row stub problems Cv solves 9 and copying 10. Of the 13 held problems Cv solves and D4v does not, D4v's answer is unfinished on 8, wrong on 4, unparsable on 1, a stub on none; of the 54 such row problems, wrong 18, unfinished 27, a stub 9. So the stub alone does not explain D4v's distance from Cv on new problems.
**Reading (a hypothesis).** The fit's target at every position is E4B's whole next-token distribution. Where E4B leans to the stub, the lean is in its states; the corrector fed those states can reproduce it problem by problem, and the self-corrector, which never sees them, learns only the average, where the stub is rare. Clean text does not remove it, because the target is not the text.
**The probe (209d's, extended; transfer/stub_probe.py, colab/xq_stubprobe.sh with code10; one G4 session, forward passes and a few samples).** On the 234 LeetCode-format problems with a one-line signature (21 where D4v at seed 1 wrote the stub; E4B's own greedy answer is a stub on 29): the log-probability of each token of the stub's body after the prompt and the signature under E2B alone, E4B, base E2B corrected by 209g's cross-model corrector and by 209a's (the untested data), base E2B corrected by 209g's self-corrector, and the seed-1 students D4v, S4v, Cv; and 32 on-policy samples per writer. The reading holds if, on the 21 problems, E4B gives the stub's body a higher probability than E2B alone and the cross-model teacher more than the self-corrector's, and would write it itself (every body token its most likely one) on more of them than the self-corrector's teacher. If E4B does not favour the stub there and the cross-model teacher does, the stub is the corrector's own doing and the fit below is not expected to remove it.
**The fit with verified-token targets (online.py ON_FIT_HARD=1, code11; colab/xq_lcbv.sh FIT_HARD=1; launcher dreamer/xq_lcbh_launch.sh).** The user's rule for the text ("train it on actual good tasks that succeed") applied to the target: on the 608 judged rows of 209g, at every position where E4B's own top token is not the token the verified solution has next, the target is that token; at all other positions and on the general text it is E4B's distribution as before. Everything else as 209g: the same rows, weights, steps and smoke test; then D4 taught with that corrector only on answers that pass, seeds 1 and 2 (sessions xqyFitD, xqyD1, xqyD2; arm onl_d4w). The fit does not wait for the probe: its sessions take the slots as 209g's second seeds end, and the probe takes a free one.
**Rules.** (1) "Verified-token targets remove the stub" if D4w's student writes it on fewer than 10 of the 487 problems in each seed. (2) "They make the corrector route the best on new problems" only if D4w solves more held problems than Cv in each seed and the pooled sign test has p < 0.05; "it matches Cv" if the pooled test does not reach p < 0.05 and the pooled difference is within 10 per seed; "Cv stays ahead" if Cv beats it at p < 0.05. (3) Against D4v, the same corrector route with E4B's distribution as the target: pooled sign test on held problems and on row problems, lost problems beside it. One seed supports no ranking.
**Predictions.** Probe: E4B gives the stub a higher probability than E2B alone on at least 14 of the 21 problems (65%); the cross-model teacher more than the self-corrector's teacher on at least 14 (70%). Fit: E4B's top token differs from the verified text's next token at 10% to 30% of the positions (60%); the fitted teacher is closer to E4B than E2B alone on the held coding rows (90%) and less close than 209g's 0.315 (70%). Teaching: rule 1 is met (60%); D4w loses fewer solved row problems than D4v's 17 at seed 1 (60%); D4w solves at least 75 held problems at seed 1 (50%), at least 79 (20%); rule 2's "the best" (7%), "matches" (65%).
**Why the other problems fail (user, 05:50: "why did the other tasks fail?"; stored answers, no new run).** On the problems Cv solves and D4v does not at seed 1 (13 held, 54 row), D4v's answer is unfinished on 35: about 30 comment lines and 18 code lines, cut inside a comment in 27; Cv's passing answers there have 17 comment lines and 23 to 29 code lines. Answers with five or more thinking-aloud comment lines, of 487: E2B alone 61, D4v 31, S4v 10, Cv 13; unfinished answers 208, 139, 142, 116. E4B's own greedy answers on the 188 held problems: 74 pass, 86 are unfinished, 17 are stubs, 11 are wrong or do not parse: E4B fails by running out of room or by the stub, and the student that follows its states keeps more of both. The 22 wrong answers (4 held, 18 row) have the passing answers' shape; no pattern is seen in them.
**Review (a subagent).** No fault in the tensor logic, the job script's cases, the launcher or the probe's additions. Taken: at a position where E4B and E2B alone pick the same token and the verified text has another, the do-no-harm term no longer pulls back to E2B's own distribution (it worked against the new target there); the job's check that the fit used the new target now needs a non-zero count.
**Limits.** One fit; two seeds; the target change is one of several that could act on the stub (a self-corrector's veto, a corrector that reads both models' states); no self-corrector twin with the new target is run yet.

## Amendment 209i (06:01, Nov 11; the first reading is done, the rest not yet run): rows with long rambling comments are regenerated by resampling, DeepSeek judging
User, 05:57: "we should also maybe regenerate tasks tyst have long rambaling comments via resampling and using deepseek to judge/verify/ask for resample"; 06:02, on the plan: "by teach one seed... is it still going to be 4x corrector?" (yes: the corrector is the 4x fit, 2,400 steps, every setting as 209g; a seed is one teaching run of a student with it).
**Why.** 209g's judge was told that many comments are fine, and 44 of its 608 rows are half comments or more; unfinished answers (reasoning in comments up to the 768-token cap) are the largest failure of every arm and of E4B itself (86 of its 188 greedy held answers).
**First reading, done (judge_rows.py --direct; DeepSeek V4.1 Flash, two votes; 1,216 calls, $0.48).** Each of 209g's 608 fit rows is read with numbered lines: written directly (code is the bulk; comments short) or rambling (a comment block of more than about six lines that restates the problem, plans in prose, analyses constraints or traces examples; comments that repeat the code line by line; more comment than code; a docstring that reasons about the task), with the first rambling line. A trial on 8 rows came first ($0.008; the prompt was not changed after it). Result: direct in both votes 269; rambling in both 227 (E4B 164, E2B 63); split 112. Mean comment-line share 0.26, 0.42 and 0.33.
**Regeneration (as 209g's fix step; transfer/lcb_fix.py FIX_MODE=continue; one G4 session, xqzFix).** The 227 rows both votes call rambling: the author model (E4B for E4B's rows, E2B for E2B's) gets the plain prompt and its own solution up to just before the first rambling line (the earlier of the two votes; the median row keeps 21% of its lines) and samples 2 continuations, and 2 fresh answers to the plain prompt, at temperature 0.8. Every version is run on its tests. For each row the three shortest versions that pass, end with their fence and pass the rules of form are read by the judge: directness first (two votes), and those direct in both votes for cleanness with 209g's text (two votes). The shortest version accepted in all four votes replaces the row. A both-votes-rambling row with no accepted version is left out; a split row is kept as it is (lcb_fit_rows.py final with FIT_KEEP_SPLIT=1).
**Then.** The 4x cross-model corrector is fitted on these rows with 209h's verified-token targets (2,400 steps, 209g's weights, the smoke test first) and D4 is taught with it at seed 1 (arm onl_d4x), beside 209h's two seeds on the rows as they are. Seed 2 goes to the better of the two by stubs, unfinished answers and held solved; the choice is made on one seed and is reported as that.
**Predictions.** At least 55% of the 227 rows get an accepted version (50%). The final file holds at least 500 rows (55%) with a mean comment-line share under 0.30 (65%). D4x at seed 1 leaves fewer answers unfinished than 209g's D4v (139 of 487) (60%) and solves at least as many held problems as 209h's D4w at seed 1 (50%).
**Limits.** The judge's standard of "direct" is one prompt; one resampling round; the rows the teaching stream falls back on (E4B's shortest verified solution per problem) are not regenerated; one seed before the choice.

**209i, self-rewrites become candidates too (07:26, Nov 11).** User, 07:05: "would asking the models themsleves to correct their own completed work and fix it be a valid way to get the tasks to pass and still be a goood training target?"; 07:22: "if we have the models that wrote the bad/overlong [tasks] correct them itself, by telling it why its bad, would that count as a valid task to train it on? Would it cause any issues?"
- Measured on what was already stored: 209g's instructed rewrites (xqxFix; set aside then as "written to another prompt") were put through the same three readings (the three shortest passing versions per row, 380 versions of 142 rows; $0.40): direct in both votes 328 (136 rows); of those of reasonable length 218 (105 rows); of those clean 215 (104 rows). So 104 of 142 rows (73%) have a rewrite accepted in all six votes, against 22% and 17% for the two resampling batches. The accepted rewrites are a median 0.39 of the original's length, with a comment-line share of 0.16; 45 of the 215 hold no comment line at all.
- Judgement, corrected from 209g's: a self-rewrite is a valid row if the feedback names the kind of fault and not a way to solve the task. The with-a-plan rows were left out because the plan carries E4B's solution ideas into E2B's answer; a clean-up instruction carries none: the algorithm is the one the model found from the plain prompt, the model writes the text, the tests are run again, the judge reads it again. Risks named to the user: (1) the judge's free-text reasons sometimes hold a solution idea ("can be replaced by summing the two smallest prices"), and a rewrite that follows one teaches what the model did not find; (2) the comments may be how these models reason, so direct answers may cost hard problems what they gain on the token cap; (3) a student trained on its own tidied text grows more confident (the keep term; drift is reported); (4) rewrites that drop every comment are further from either model's own style (the best-of judge is told to prefer a few short comments over none).
- So: a self-rewrite session for the 298 unresolved rows (xqzRW; lcb_fix.py FIX_MODE=rewrite: a greedy rewrite and two samples per row by its own author, "keep the same algorithm and the same behaviour"), its passing versions joining the 16 resamples and the original as candidates for the best-of judge. Against risk 1 the model is given only the category and the line ("its comments ramble ..., from about line N on"; "its code is longer than the problem needs ..., from about line N on"), never the judge's reasons; the session's first inputs, which held the reasons, were replaced before it started.
- All three resampling shards run (xqzR160 07:01, xqzR161 07:21, xqzR162 07:26); 209g's last session ended at 07:25.

**209i, the fit and teaching plan, registered before the picks are judged (07:35, Nov 11).** User, 07:31: "we need to hury up so if this can be sped up by putting it on the collab runners, that is fine also". Everything heavy already runs on Colab, and Colab refuses a fourth session (06:13: "Allocation refused ... too many active sessions"), so the order is what can be changed.

(4) Cross against self: D4x against S4x by the pooled sign test on held and on row problems, the sign in each seed beside it; "the cross-model corrector adds to the self-corrector" only if D4x is ahead in each seed and the pooled test has p < 0.05. Two seeds of one recipe have differed by 10 to 12 held problems; a difference of that size in one seed supports nothing.

## Amendment 209l (08:12, Nov 11; built, in review, not yet run): the stub direction erased from what the cross-model corrector reads
User, 07:47, on 209k: "well it seems to pick up the habits and both good and bad form. (so maybe its less style and more unconsicous proccessing)"; 07:51: "if possible we should try to somehow fix the bad habbits of thea teacher also? Not sure how we can do that... maybe by finetuning the teacher itself, or is there a way of using the probe to 'fix' this bad behavour? Can you do something like this yet? https://rikiyat.github.io/blog/weight-space-programming/"; 07:56, on the three routes offered (erase the direction from the corrector's input; edit E4B's weights; fine-tune E4B on its own verified direct answers): "yes, try option 1 first".
**The post** (Takehi, Teoh, Zhang and Khattab, "Sparks of Weight-Space Programming", Nov 2, 2026; read today, not checked beyond the post): a model reads and writes weights directly. Its one language-model edit traces repetitive looping in Qwen3.5-0.8B to one direction written by the MLPs of two layers and subtracts it from those weights, with a Python kernel and 50 forward passes; token-limit hits fall from 93% to 33%. Its own caveat: an edit removes an obstacle and does not add a missing ability. The part that can be done here is that one: find a direction, remove it, measure.
**Why it could work.** The student never saw a stub in its teaching text (only answers that pass; verified fit rows), copying E4B's output distribution on the same text leaves none (Cv 0, 0), and every fitted cross-model corrector's student writes it (72, 67, 21; 209k). So the habit reaches the student through what the corrector reads in E4B's states. If it is one direction there, it can be taken out of the corrector's input and E4B is left as it is.
**Method.**
- The direction (transfer/stub_dir.py, colab/xq_erase.sh, session xqyDir; forward passes, no training). On the 404 LeetCode-format problems with a one-line signature among E4B's 901 stored greedy answers (316 in the train pool, 88 held; E4B's own answer is a stub on 56 and 17), E4B reads the prompt and the same text for every problem, "from typing import List / class Solution: / its signature". The states of the corrector's two source layers at the last position are whitened with the corrector's own statistics (identical in every G4 refit: 209a, 209e and 209g agree exactly). The label is what E4B does next there: its 12-token greedy continuation starts the stub. The direction is the difference of the class means on the train-pool problems, unit length; erasing it sets the component along it to the no-stub mean (the two means are then equal on those problems, so no linear reader separates them there).
- The cause, on E4B itself, in the same session: each source layer's part of the direction erased in E4B's own residual stream; how many held stub problems still continue with the stub, against a random direction.
- The corrector (colab/xq_lcbv.sh with ERASEDIR; code12 = code11 with translator.Tr.set_erase and online.py ON_ERASE; session xqyFitE): 209g's fit again, one thing changed: the same 608 rows, E4B's distribution as the target, 2,400 steps, the direction erased from its input. 209g's configuration and not 209i's because its student's stubs are known (D4v, 21 at seed 1).
- Teaching (session xqyE1, seed 1): D4 with that corrector and the erasure on, only on answers that pass, as D4v. Launcher dreamer/xq_lcbl_launch.sh (xq_lcbi_launch.sh's reviewed helpers); it takes the third slot beside 209i's two cross-model seeds; the self-corrector's seeds follow.
**Rules.** (1) The fit runs only if the direction separates the held problems with AUC of at least 0.80. (2) "Erasing the direction removes the stub from the student" if xqyE1's student writes it on fewer than 10 of the 487 problems (D4v 21); S4v's two seeds gave 0 and 10, so fewer than 5 is clear and 5 to 15 is not. (3) "At no cost" if it solves at least 65 held problems (D4v 75; two seeds of one recipe differ by 10 to 12) and the fitted teacher is as close to E4B on the held coding rows as 209g's (0.315) within 0.01. (4) "The direction carries the habit in E4B itself" if at most half of the held stub problems still continue with the stub when it is erased in E4B and at least 80% do under the random direction.
**Predictions.** E4B continues with the stub on at least 40 of the 316 train-pool problems (70%). Held AUC at least 0.80 (65%), at least 0.90 (40%). Rule 4 met (35%: two layers are erased and later layers may write it again). The refitted teacher within 0.01 of 0.315 (75%). Rule 2 met (50%), fewer than 5 stubs (35%). At least 70 held problems solved (60%).
**Limits.** One direction, linear, found at one position and erased at every position; a small class (tens of problems); one seed; labels at a prefix written for E4B, not its own; the unfinished answers, E4B's larger failure, are not touched.

**209l, added before anything ran (08:22, Nov 11): with and without the erasure for both correctors; the review.** User, 08:15: "we shuld try both the one with the directoin fix, and one without it".
- Two pairs, the erasure the only difference inside each: (a) 209g's corrector (the 608 rows, E4B's distribution as the target): without = D4v (seed 1: 21 stubs, 75 held), with = xqyFitE and xqyE1; (b) 209i's corrector (the 602 regenerated rows, verified-token targets): without = D4x (xqzD1, xqzD2), with = xqyFitX and xqyX1 at seed 1 (files in data/lcb/lx/). Pair (a) has a baseline known to write the stub; pair (b) is the corrector the paper would report. Order in the one free slot: the direction, both fits, xqyE1; xqyX1 when 209i's first seeds end. Rules 2 and 3 are read on pair (a) as written and on pair (b) against D4x at seed 1; if D4x itself writes the stub on fewer than 10 problems, pair (b) says nothing about the stub and only shows whether the erasure costs anything. Predictions for pair (b): xqyX1 writes no more stubs than D4x at seed 1 (70%) and solves within 10 held problems of it (75%).
- Review (a subagent; the tensor maths was run on the real corrector class with 209g's checkpoint on CPU). One fault that would have ended the fit session: the small test fit takes its whitening statistics from 6 calibration points, so set_erase's check against the full fit's statistics would fail there; the small fit now skips that check (the full fit and every teaching pass keep it; 209e's and 209g's full-fit statistics are identical). One reading without meaning: a second difference of means after the erasure is rounding error by construction; replaced by a ridge probe fitted on the train-pool problems and read on the held ones, before and after the erasure (on synthetic data with a planted direction: 0.88 before, 0.44 after). Noted, not changed: older analysis loaders in online.py would read an erased corrector without the erasure; none is used here. No fault found in the hooks, the erase file, the job scripts' use of the erasure or the launcher's gates.

## Amendments 209m and 209n (08:49, Nov 11; built, in review, not yet run): what follows the stub, and the verified text as the target where the student fails
User, 08:44: "so how can we remove it from e2b?" (the stub that is left in the cross-model corrector's student is E2B's own; 209h's probe).
**209m, what follows the stub (transfer/stub_past.py, colab/xq_stubpast.sh; one short G4 session, no training).** Each writer gets the prompt and its own stored stub answer to the closing fence and goes on greedily for up to 768 tokens with no stop at a fence; a second fenced block is run on the problem's tests. Writers and their stub answers on the 487 problems: E4B 29 (17 held), E2B alone 26 (11 held), D4v's student at seed 1, 21 (11 held). Reading: "the stub is a skeleton before an answer" for a writer if a second block follows at least half of its stubs; how many pass is reported beside the registered measure (held solved, cut at the first fence) as a second reading, for these writers only; it replaces nothing. Predictions: a second block follows at least 25 of E4B's 29 (85%) and at least 10 of the 29 pass (55%); at least 13 of E2B alone's 26 (50%), at least 4 pass (40%); at least 10 of the student's 21 (50%), at least 3 pass (40%).
**209n, the verified text where the student fails (online.py ON_VERIFY_HARD; colab/xq_lcbv.sh VERIFY_HARD=1; code13).** In 209e to 209i the student learns on its own samples that pass and, when none passes, on E4B's verified solution of the problem toward the teacher's distribution on it. That happened on 351 of D4v's 747 learned items (47%; S4v 362 and 345; Cv 286 and 292). Where the teacher is base E2B corrected, its distribution on that text keeps E2B's own probabilities for what E2B likes to write there, the stub among them, so nothing pushes them down; a copying teacher's target has no room for them. Change: on those items the target is the verified solution's own tokens (cross-entropy, as copying); on the student's passing samples the corrected teacher teaches as before. The arm: D4 with 209i's cross-model corrector (xqzFitD) and this fallback, seed 1, started when 209i's first seeds end, whatever they show (their stubs are not yet known); a second seed only if the first writes fewer stubs than D4x at seed 1 or solves at least as many held problems. It is a hybrid, named as one: copying where the student fails, the corrector where it succeeds; its control is Cv (E4B's distribution, the same filter).
Rules for 209n: (1) "the fallback as text removes the stub" if its student writes it on fewer than 10 of the 487 problems; (2) "it teaches the row problems as copying does" if it fixes at least 95 of the 151 (D4v 78, S4v 82, Cv 108 and 81, copying 106); (3) held solved beside D4x's and Cv's of the same seed, lost problems beside them; no ranking from one seed. Predictions: rule 1 met (75%); rule 2 met (60%); at least 72 held problems solved (55%).
**Limits.** One seed first; half of the learned items become copying, so a gain says little about the corrector alone; 209m reads greedy continuations of stored answers, re-tokenised.

## Amendment 209o (09:03, Nov 11; built, in review, not yet run): the teacher's own corrector, fitted on the clean text
User, 08:59: "I think we will have to figure out how to use the corrector to correct the teacher model as well, if its doing something bad... maybe even use it like an inline splitter, where we train two modles at the same time, in the models own tokens, by using the cleaned text? WOuld that be possible... what I am envisioning is that we have the good training text, and use that as input ito a new 'inlet' in the corrector, that then tranlates that to each model... once we correct the issue, the larger model can then train the smaller one using the same collector?" (corrector).
**Answer given.** Possible; a corrector with the clean text as a training-time input and an outlet into each model is teaching with privileged information. What exists of it: the clean text as the target of the corrector's fit (209h's verified-token targets). What our results warn of: a correction that carries the answer is not kept by the LoRA taught from it (200: the student keeps what its own states can predict), and training toward one's own cleaned text sharpens and needs the keep term. Stages: (1) 209m, whether the stub hides an answer; (2) this amendment: a corrector that reads E4B's own states and writes into E4B, fitted on the clean rows; (3) the corrected teacher teaching E2B (the cross-model corrector refitted on its states); (4) one module with a clean-text inlet and two outlets, after the paper.
**209o (colab/xq_tfix.sh; code13 unchanged; session xqyTfix; one fit and forward passes, nobody is taught).** 209i's fit with E4B as the model written into (TR_STUDENT=e4b TR_TEACHERS=e4b): the 602 regenerated rows and the general text, the same weights and 2,400 steps, verified-token targets. Where E4B's top token is the clean text's next token the target is E4B's own distribution, so the corrector has nothing to do there; where it is not (6.1% of the coding positions in 209i's fits) the target is that token. Then stub_probe.py on 209l's 404 problems: the stub's tokens after the prompt and the signature under E4B and under E4B with its corrector.
**Rule.** "The teacher's own corrector reduces its stub on new problems" if, on the 17 held problems where E4B starts the stub (209l), the opening (the indent, "#", " ...") gets a lower probability under the corrected E4B on at least 14 (sign test p = 0.013) and the number of them where " ..." is the top token after "#" at least halves. The 54 train-pool stub problems are read beside (some of their problems have clean rows in the fit). Predictions: the rule is met (35%); on the train-pool stub problems " ..." stays the top token on at most half (60%); under the corrected E4B no problem of the other 71 held ones starts the stub that did not before (85%).
**Limits.** The stub only: E4B's larger failure, answers that run out of tokens, needs generation with the corrector on, which the cached generation path does not have yet; one fit; the corrected teacher teaches nobody here.

**209i, the cross-model student on the regenerated rows, both seeds (11:36, Nov 11; xqzD1 08:32-11:35, xqzD2 08:33-11:34).**

| answers of | held solved | fixed (of 151) | lost | stubs (of 487) | unfinished (of 487; held) | long comment runs | held, finished and wrong |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| D4x, seeds 1, 2 | 76, 75 | 92, 86 | 26, 13 | 7, 7 | 96 (71), 100 (72) | 52, 43 | 35, 36 |
| D4v (209g), seed 1 | 75 | 78 | 21 | 21 | 138 (84) | 100 | 18 |
| Cv, seeds 1, 2 | 79, 67 | 108, 81 | 8, 27 | 0, 0 | 115 (83), 157 (102) | 69, 101 | 26, 19 |
| copying E4B's verified text, seeds 1, 2 | 76, 69 | 106 (seed 1) | 12 (seed 1) | 0, 0 | 106 (84), 109 (80) | 70, 69 | 28, 39 |
| E2B alone | 57 | | | 26 | 207 (101) | 152 | 19 |

- Rules. (1) The stub: 7 and 7, under 10 in each seed: met (predicted 65%); the seven are E2B's own wording. (2) Unfinished answers at seed 1: 96, under D4v's 139 (predicted 65%: held) and under Cv's 116 (predicted 35%: held). (3) Against Cv on held problems: 8 against 11 at seed 1 (76 against 79), 17 against 9 at seed 2 (75 against 67), pooled 25 against 20 (p = 0.55): not ahead in each seed, so "the best" is not met (predicted 5%); "it matches" is met (predicted 70%). Against copying E4B's verified text pooled 25 against 19 (p = 0.45), against C 21 against 14 (p = 0.31), against E2B alone 46 against 9. On the row problems copying fixes more (pooled 84 against 43, p = 0.0003). At least 75 held at seed 1 (predicted 45%): held. (4) Cross against self waits for S4x.
- The two seeds differ by one held problem, where two seeds of the other recipes differed by 10 to 12; two seeds are no measure of that.
- What changed with the regenerated rows and verified-token targets: the answers are shorter (held median 1,834 and 1,797 characters, D4v 2,106, E2B alone 2,311), fewer run out of tokens, fewer hold long comment runs, the stub falls to a third. What it costs: more held answers that finish and are wrong (35 and 36 against D4v's 18), and at seed 2 a prose cross-entropy of 4.509 against E2B alone's 4.473 (seed 1: 4.448); drift 0.013 and 0.019; the agent gate nets -2 and +3.
- During teaching (read live at item 500): 60 to 69 of the last 120 samples pass (D4v 47 to 59 at the same items, Cv 66 to 76), fallback items 294 and 311 of 747 (D4v 351).
- User, 11:19 and 11:24, on the readings: copying damaged other generation in the offline study without an anchor; in these online runs every arm has the keep term and none is damaged (drift 0.011 to 0.016 for every teacher of 209g); so the corrector's measured advantages are speed and closeness to E4B, and "it may just teach faster, and may carry over some of e4b's calculation states to the smaller model" (user).
- Started as the slots freed: xqyPast (209m) 11:34, xqyTfix (209o) 11:35; xqzN1 (209n) and xqzS2 wait for the next two.

- S4x at seed 1: 73 held problems solved (S4v 73), 88 of 151 fixed, 20 lost, 6 stubs, 101 unfinished answers (71 held), 50 long comment runs, 39 held answers finished and wrong, held median 1,846 characters; prose cross-entropy 4.469 (E2B alone 4.473), drift 0.014, agent gate net +1. Against D4x at seed 1: 11 against 14 on held problems (73 against 76; p = 0.69), 33 against 29 on row problems. The shorter answers, the fall of the stub and of the unfinished answers are in both students: they come with the regenerated rows and the verified-token targets, and do not need E4B's states. Rule 4 waits for seed 2 (xqzS2, started 11:44).

| writer | stub answers | a second block follows | it has its closing fence | it passes | held: stubs, second block, passes |

- Reading. For E4B the rule is met: its stub is a skeleton before an answer (all 29), and the answer is right about half of the time; read past the skeleton E4B solves 78 held problems, not 74 (some second blocks are the skeleton again). For E2B alone and for the student it is not met (12 and 11 of 26 and 21): what follows is mostly prose about the problem, and one held problem each would be gained. So the stub costs E2B's students next to nothing on held problems; it marks problems they do not solve when they go on. The registered measure stays as it is (cut at the first fence), for every arm alike.
- Running: xqzN1 (209n) since 11:41, xqzS2 since 11:44, xqyTfix (209o) in its fit.

**A correction to the reading of 11:19 (11:50, Nov 11; no new run).** I told the user that copying against the corrector without the keep term "had not been run" and that the corrector's measured advantages were speed and closeness only. The agent stream says more, and for the user's point ("it improves the solving, but also does not make other thing worse"). With keep + replay, three seeds (206, Table 1): of the 168 decisions E2B alone has right, the student of E4B's distribution breaks 8, 12 and 10; the cross-model corrector's 4, 3 and 2 (4x: 4, 4, 1); the self-corrector's 3, 6 and 3. Without keep + replay, one seed (the earlier runs, Table D7): copying drifts 0.089 on untaught text and breaks 11 decisions at a held cross-entropy of 0.787; the corrector 0.055 and 4 at 0.753. So on agent decisions the corrector's students disturb less of what E2B had, in every seed, and without the keep term the one seed shows less drift. What was said holds for the online coding runs only: every arm has the keep term, drift and prose loss are alike, and the corrector's students do not lose fewer solved problems (D4x 26 and 13, Cv 8 and 27). The paper's 4.2 already reports the broken decisions.
- Paper, since 11:36: 4.5 rewritten for the online coding runs ("the corrector matches copying"), abstract, introduction, discussion and conclusion to match, Table D9 and its paragraph in Appendix D, Appendix B names the reviewer model; body 2,996 words; S4x's second seed is a marked placeholder.

## Amendment 209p (12:00, Nov 11; built, in review, not yet run): the student's own placeholder answers made less likely
User, 11:55: "so there are pros and cons for both it seems like copying is better ins omethings and the collector at others?" (yes: the corrector is faster, closer, breaks fewer of E2B's right decisions and leaves fewer answers unfinished; copying fixes more training problems, removes the stub and is simpler; held coding problems and live tasks tie); 11:58: "cant we use negative reinforcement to tell the model not to do the stub?"
**What.** In 209e to 209i the tests only select: the student learns on its samples that pass and ignores the others. 209p adds a penalty on one kind of failure (online.py ON_STUB_NEG; colab/xq_lcbv.sh STUB_NEG; code14): when a sample of the student is a placeholder answer (it has its closing fence and its last line of code is a def with nothing under it; dreamer/lcb_failkinds.py's rule, the same on all 2,435 stored answers checked), the tokens from after the comment sign to the closing fence (" ... implementation", the line end, the fence) get an unlikelihood loss, -log(1 - p), averaged over those tokens, times 0.5 (after review computed as the log of the other tokens' mass, with no clamp: a clamp at 0.99 would have left the tokens the student is surest of, the ones that make the habit, without any push; the gradient on a token's logit is p, at most 1, and the step is clipped as always). The indent and the comment sign are left alone: a real body may start with them. No other failing answer is penalised. The arm: D4 with 209i's cross-model corrector (xqzFitD), everything else as D4x, seed 1 (session xqzP1), beside 209n's arm, which shows the right text where 209p forbids the wrong one.
**Rules.** (1) "The penalty removes the stub" if the student writes it on at most 2 of the 487 problems (D4x 7 and 7). (2) "At no cost" if it solves at least 66 held problems (D4x 76; seeds differ by up to 12) and leaves no more than 115 answers unfinished (D4x 96 and 100). Predictions: at least 30 placeholder samples are penalised during the 598 items (70%); rule 1 met (60%); held solved within 5 of 76 (50%). 209m says not to expect more held problems solved from this.
**Limits.** One seed; one weight; the penalty names one form of failure, found by a rule; where the probability goes is left to the teacher and the keep term.

**209o read: the teacher's own corrector lowers its stub on new problems (12:06, Nov 11; xqyTfix 11:35-12:05).**
- The fit (E4B written into, its own states read; 2,400 steps in 967 s; peak memory 26 GiB in the small pass): verified-token targets at 36,636 of 600,795 coding positions (6.1%, the same positions as in 209i's fits); at the last step the gate is 0.04 and the change size 0.019: a small correction. The probe's check: the corrector moves a stub token's log-probability by up to 3.5; the two copies of E4B agree exactly.
- The stub after the prompt and the signature, E4B plain against E4B with its corrector (teacher-forced; 209l's 404 problems and labels):

| problems | the opening's log-probability (indent, "#", " ...") | " ..." is the top token after "#" | the whole opening is the top choice at each step |
|---|---|---|---|
| held, E4B starts the stub (17) | -1.21 to -2.17; lower on 16 | 17 to 6 | 17 to 3 |
| train pool, E4B starts the stub (54) | -1.31 to -2.33; lower on 54 | 51 to 24 | 51 to 18 |
| held, it does not (71) | -4.47 to -5.06; lower on 55 | 19 to 11 | 0 to 0 |
| train pool, it does not (262) | -3.79 to -4.48; lower on 211 | 83 to 52 | 2 to 2 |

- Rule: lower on at least 14 of the 17 (16; sign test p = 0.0003) and the top-token count at least halved (17 to 6): met (predicted 35%). The train-pool stub problems: " ..." stays the top token on 24 of 51, under half (predicted 60%: held). No held problem of the other 71 starts the stub that did not before (predicted 85%: held; two train-pool ones do, as two others stop).
- Reading. A corrector fitted on the clean text, with nothing but the model's own states as its input, changes what E4B is about to do on problems it never saw: the stub's opening is no longer its first choice on 14 of 17. 209l's single direction left 9 of 17. This is the first step of the user's "inlet" (stage 2 of 209o's list). Not shown: what the corrected E4B generates (finished answers, solved problems), for which generation with the corrector on is needed; and stage 3, for which one process would have to hold two models' layouts (translator.py's constants are per process).
- 209p: the review found that a clamp at 0.99 took the gradient off the tokens the student is surest of; the loss is now the log of the other tokens' mass (the same value, a gradient of p on the token's logit). xqzP1 started 12:06 (code14).

## Amendment 209q (12:09, Nov 11; built, in review, not yet run): what the corrected teacher writes
209o read the corrected E4B's next-token choices; this lets it answer. transfer/tfix_gen.py, colab/xq_tfixgen.sh (code13 unchanged; session xqyTgen; no training): E4B with 209o's corrector on answers 77 held problems greedily, to the closing fence or 768 tokens, by a loop of two passes per token without a cache (its own states, then the pass with the correction written, as online.control_lp). The problems, by what E4B's stored greedy answer is: all 17 placeholder answers, 40 of the 86 unfinished ones and 20 of the 74 that pass (drawn with seed 209). Every answer is run on the problem's tests; on the first three problems the loop also runs with the corrector off, to see that it writes E4B's stored answer.
**Rules.** "The corrected teacher writes better answers" if (1) at most 8 of the 17 are still placeholder answers, (2) of the 57 problems E4B fails more are now solved than of the 20 it solves are lost, and (3) at least 17 of the 20 still pass. Predictions: rule 1 (65%); at least 3 of the 17 pass (40%); at least 10 of the 40 unfinished answers now finish (50%) and at least 4 of them pass (40%); rule 3 (60%).
**Limits.** 77 problems, one fit of the corrector, greedy answers; the stored answers were generated with a cache, the loop's are not (the check on three problems reads that difference); nobody is taught by the corrected teacher here.

## Addendum 177b (13:36, Nov 11; built, in review, not yet run): the RSI pilot's arms again with what 209 taught
User, 12:48: "what is running on the 4090?" (the pilot of 177, waiting since Nov 7 behind the earlier 4090 jobs; it started at 07:24 today); 13:27: "is it actually improving?"; 13:28: "we might have to update the script also, with what we learned about which method for training works best with what settings, can you check?"; 13:30: "update the script and then start it after this one finishes, and we can compare this one, and the new one, and maybe learn from both".
**The pilot so far (Qwen3-1.7B, seed 1; 517 train-pool and 188 held problems; model 0 solves 98 and 24).** S:U1 (cross-entropy on its own wins), rounds 1 to 3: train pool 216, 249, 270 (greedy passes of models 1 to 3); held 37, 43, 40 (new 15, 24, 20 of 164; kept 22, 19, 20 of 24); against model 0 at round 3 on held problems 20 against 4 (p = 0.0015); no stubs. S:U5 (on-policy toward the self-corrected model, on every sample of the round): train pool 102, 111, 130; held 24, 25, 28 (new 6, 7, 10; kept 18 of 24 in every round; kept train 69, 69, 71 of 98); against model 0 on held 10 against 6 (p = 0.45); stub share 5.3%, 9.6%, 3.7%. T:U1 and T:U5 follow tonight.
**What differs from what worked in 209.** A win is the first passing attempt, not the shortest (176, 209i: shorter, direct rows teach best and leave fewer answers unfinished). U1 has no pull to the previous model (181 to 193: a light KL removes the harm of training on one's own text at no cost). U5 learns on failing samples as well as passing ones (209e on: only answers that pass), has no keep term (206: keep + replay is what holds the student in place) and a translator fitted for 600 steps (199: 2,400).
**The updated arms (rsi.py, commit f09822a6; dreamer/rsi4090b_run.sh; the 4090 after the pilot ends, about 22:00).** RSI_WIN=shortest for all four. S:U2 and T:U2: cross-entropy on the wins + 0.3 x KL to the previous model over the written span. S:U6 and T:U6: the translator fitted on the wins for 2,400 steps; the LoRA learns on the model's own attempts of the round that pass (greedy and samples) toward the corrected model, on the stronger model's rows as text (T), and every step on one row of untaught text (train-split prose, code, stories; 300 rows) with KL(student || model r-1) x 1.0. Everything else is the pilot's (seed, pools, K 4, 400 update steps, 3 rounds, the sandbox); model 0 and its round-1 attempts are the pilot's own (the same folder), so the old and new arms are paired problem by problem. Each arm changes more than one thing against its old twin (U2: the win rule and the KL; U6: the filter, the keep term, the longer fit, the win rule, the text rows): the comparison says whether the bundle is better, not which part.
**Rules (at round 3, against the old twin of the same source).** (1) "The rebuilt on-policy rule holds what the model had" if S:U6 keeps at least 110 of the 122 problems model 0 solved (S:U5 89; S:U1 118). (2) "It learns new problems" if S:U6 solves at least 15 new held problems (S:U5 10; S:U1 20) and is ahead of S:U5 on the held problems both model 0s failed (sign test p < 0.1). (3) "The updated plain rule is no worse" if S:U2 solves at least 15 new held problems and keeps at least 20 of the 24 (S:U1 20 and 20). (4) Stub share of S:U6 at most 2% in every round (S:U5 3.7% to 9.6%). The T arms are read the same way against T:U1 and T:U5 when those exist. Predictions: rule 1 (70%); rule 2 (35%); rule 3 (60%); rule 4 (60%); S:U6 ahead of S:U1 on new held problems at p < 0.1 (8%).
**Limits.** One seed, one student, three rounds; bundles, not single changes; the keep term's text is from the same store the off-task measure's held rows come from (other rows).

**209n read at seed 1: the corrector where the student succeeds, the verified text where it fails (14:44, Nov 11; xqzN1 11:41-14:41).** User, 14:43: "is this still using the corrector still though? If so, record the pattern and add it to the paper". It is: on the 486 of 747 learned items where a sample of the student passes, the teacher is base E2B corrected by 209i's cross-model corrector; on the other 261 (35%; 257 learned as text) the target is E4B's verified solution itself.

| answers of (seed 1) | held solved | fixed (of 151) | lost | stubs | unfinished (held) | long comment runs | held, finished and wrong | held median chars |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| D4n: the hybrid (209n) | 80 | 99 | 12 | 0 | 116 (83) | 69 | 25 | 2,028 |
| D4x: 209i's cross-model corrector | 76 | 92 | 26 | 7 | 96 (71) | 52 | 35 | 1,834 |
| Cv: E4B's distribution, passing answers only | 79 | 108 | 8 | 0 | 115 (83) | 69 | 26 | |
| copying E4B's verified text | 76 | 106 | 12 | 0 | 106 (84) | 70 | 28 | |

- Rules: (1) the stub: 0 of 487, under 10: met (predicted 75%). (2) fixed at least 95 of 151: 99, met (predicted 60%). (3) held solved 80 beside D4x's 76 and Cv's 79; by problem against D4x 13 against 9 on held (p = 0.52) and 45 against 28 on row problems (p = 0.06), against Cv 11 against 10 and 25 against 39, against copying 16 against 12 and 20 against 32; lost 12 (D4x 26). At least 72 held (predicted 55%): held. No ranking from one seed.
- During teaching 750 of 1,494 samples pass (D4x 686, Cv 725). Prose cross-entropy 4.472 (E2B alone 4.473), drift 0.016, agent gate net +3 (5 fixed, 2 broken).
- The pattern, as recorded for the paper. Copying shows a whole right answer and is what teaches a problem the student cannot yet do; the corrected student teaches in the student's own text and is what refines an answer it can already write. Each is used where the tests say it applies: a passing sample is refined, a failed item is shown. At this seed the hybrid has the most held problems solved of any run (80), the fewest lost with copying (12), no stub, and more row problems fixed than the corrector alone (99 against 92). What it gives back: the answers are as long and as often unfinished as the copying students' (116 unfinished, held median 2,028 characters, against D4x's 96 and 1,834): the text it copies is E4B's verified solution as E4B wrote it, not a rewritten one (209i's limit). Copying the rewritten solutions there is the next step.
- By the registered rule (80 is at least D4x's 76) seed 2 runs: xqzN2, queued 14:43 with seed 1's frozen job script and code13.
- 209i: S4x at seed 2 solves 75 held problems by its evaluation log (seed 1: 73); its files are still to come. 209p: 3 placeholder samples were penalised in the whole run (13 tokens; predicted at least 30: failed): the student samples the stub too rarely for the penalty to act.

**Agreement between seeds (not registered; stored results; 14:50, Nov 11).** User, 14:49: "The cross model corrector (outside the hybrid) seems to be more consistent now". Read by problem on the 188 held ones: D4x's two seeds solve 68 problems in both and differ on 15 (8 and 7); S4x 60 in both, 28 differ; Cv 59, 28; C 63, 18; copying E4B's verified text 62, 21; S4v (the earlier fit) 55, 26. Problems where only one student's seeds differ: S4x 21 against D4x 8 (sign test p = 0.024); Cv 21 against 8 (p = 0.024); C 11 against 8 (p = 0.65); copying 16 against 10 (p = 0.33). On the row problems every student's seeds differ on 58 to 67. So the cross-model corrector's student is the most repeatable on new problems in this one pair of seeds, more than the self-corrector's, whose equal totals (73, 75) hide twice the turnover. Chosen after seeing the totals, one pair of seeds, four comparisons: a lead. A guess at why: the cross-model teacher's targets rest on E4B's states, which do not depend on the student's seed; the self-corrector's rest on the student's own.

## Addendum 177c, part 1 (16:45, Nov 11; the judge's check done, its picks not yet run): the hybrid loop with E4B as the teacher, and a larger judge for the text it copies
User, 16:25: "once the RSI loop stops today on the 4090, we should try this version of it... on the smaller model and the e4b (using the e4b as the teacher)"; 16:37: "we need to use a better/larger judge though"; 16:38: "use the openrouter deepseek as the judge", "or, maybe the local qwen 3.8 flash next"; 16:41: "I added 10 more to the openrouter".
**Reading.** "This version" is the hybrid update inside the loop (rsi.py U6 with source T: the model's own passing attempts refined toward the corrected model, a stronger model's verified solution as text where no own attempt passes, a keep term). For Qwen3-1.7B that is 177b's T:U6, queued, with E4B's verified rows as the text and the model's own corrector. New: the same loop with E2B as the student and E4B teaching both ways (its states feed the corrector, refitted each round; its verified solution is the copied text). The loop's code and launcher are being built (a separate agent; nothing started); their design, rules and predictions follow in part 2 before anything runs.
**The judge.** The copied text in 209r was chosen by DeepSeek V4.1 Flash 1017. For the loop a larger judge reads every problem again: DeepSeek V4 Pro 0813 on OpenRouter (reasoning effort medium; the 4090 is taken by the loop all night, so the local Qwen3.8-Flash-Next is left for a second reading when the card is free). dreamer/lcb_bestall.py: per training problem (the 299 of E4B's stream rows) the candidates are E4B's stream row and the problem's other verified texts (E4B's and E2B's own passing solutions and their authors' passing rewrites and resamples from 209g-209i; one closing fence, the rules of form), the stream row and the seven shortest others, shortest first: 299 groups, median 6 candidates, 36 with the stream row alone (all_groups.jsonl sha256 eb1f6d6a...). judge_rows.py --best (209i's prompt: pick the best example to learn from and say whether it is acceptable), two votes. A row is written only if both votes call their pick acceptable; different picks: the shorter text. The judge selects and never writes; a problem with no acceptable pick keeps E4B's stream row in the loop.
**The judge's check, set before it ran.** 39 groups with one planted fault among the real candidates: 12 with a placeholder answer of untrained E2B on that problem (the shortest candidate in 11), 11 with a 209e row whose comments name a plan, 16 with a solution that the word list calls thinking aloud and both Flash votes of 209i's first reading called rambling. Gate: no vote may pick a placeholder or plan row and call it acceptable; at most 2 of the 32 thinking-aloud votes may; no vote missing.
**Result of the check (78 calls, $0.37).** Placeholder: picked in 0 of 24 votes. Thinking aloud: picked in 0 of 32. Plan rows: picked in 4 of 22 votes, called not acceptable in all 4, so none would be written. Gate passed.
**Predictions for the picks (598 calls).** Both votes accept a pick on at least 240 of the 299 problems (65%). On the 263 problems with a choice the pick is not E4B's stream row on at least 60% (60%); the two votes pick the same text on at least half (55%). The median pick is at most 0.8 times the length of the stream row (60%). The pick is the text 209r copies (the shortest row of the Flash-judged file) on at least 40% of the problems that have one (50%). Spend at most $4 (80%).
**Limits.** One judge model, one prompt, two votes; "best to learn from" is the judge's standard, checked only against three planted kinds of fault; the candidates were already filtered by the Flash judge's rounds (rewrites exist only for rows it flagged).

**209i's scorecard tally, redone (16:48, Nov 11; a subagent listed every registered prediction with its line and outcome).** 24 prediction sentences on four lines, 22 distinct (two are restated). Marked in the log so far: 10 held, 5 failed, 7 not scored. Five of the seven are decided by numbers already here or in the stored files: mean comment-line share of the final file under 0.30: held (0.257 over the 602 rows); the final file holds 400 to 520 rows: failed (602; registered before the 400-row floor was replaced); at least 70% of the unresolved rows get an acceptable pick: held (298 of 298, after the authors' rewrites joined the candidates); the pick is shorter than the original for at least 80%: held (297 of 298, from picks.jsonl); by a median of at least 20%: held (median ratio 0.45); the final file holds at least 480 rows: held (602). Undecidable: at least as many held problems as 209h's D4w at seed 1 (D4w was not run). Tally now: 15 of 22 held, 6 failed, 1 not scored; the paper's row is changed. 209g's row (8 of 11 held, 3 not scored) was checked against its 11 predictions and stands.
