## Addendum 177c, part 2 (18:13, Nov 11; built and reviewed, queued, not yet run): the hybrid loop with E2B as the student and E4B as the teacher
User, 16:25: "once the RSI loop stops today on the 4090, we should try this version of it... on the smaller model and the e4b (using the e4b as the teacher)". Read as: the hybrid update of 209n and 209r inside the loop of 177. For Qwen3-1.7B that is 177b's T:U6 (E4B's verified rows as text, the self-corrector), which runs with the rest of 177b after this loop (the new loop goes first after the pilot, so that a failure of its small pass on the real models shows while it can still be fixed tonight). New here: the same loop with Gemma 4 E2B as the student and E4B teaching both ways, through its states and through its text.
**The loop (rsi.py rule U7; dreamer/rsi4090c_run.sh; the 4090, when the pilot ends).** Seed 1, 3 rounds, the pilot's pools (517 train-pool problems, 238 easy and 279 medium; 188 held), K 4, 400 update steps of 2 rows, rank 64, RSI_WIN=shortest. Per round: model r-1 answers every train-pool problem once greedily and four times sampled; the tests pick the wins. The translator is fitted again on that round's wins for 2,400 steps, its source E4B's states at 50% and 65% depth on the same tokens (one tokenizer, identity alignment). The LoRA then learns (a) on every distinct attempt of model r-1 that passes, toward model r-1 corrected by that translator; (b) on a problem with no own win, a verified solution as text: the one DeepSeek V4.1 Flash picked for that problem among its verified solutions where there is one (209r's rule; user, 17:19: "I think we should also use flash 1017 (4.1, insread of 4 pro)"; part 1), E4B's row as first written otherwise; (c) every step, one row of untaught text with KL(student || model r-1) x 1.0. The LoRA is merged; the merged model is measured on both pools.
- The text. E4B's file (xl_rows.jsonl, sha256 b6de4379...) has a verified row for 296 of the 517 train-pool problems. 267 of them take the judge's pick (runs/xql/best177c/xl_rows_best_flash.jsonl, sha256 c789744f..., made by dreamer/lcb_bestall.py: one row per problem, two votes that must both accept; 270 rows, 3 for hard problems outside this pool): 211 written by E4B and 56 by E2B; 26 are E4B's row itself, 239 are shorter than it; median 736 characters. The other 29 keep E4B's row (median 1,740 characters). The loop's rule is still "the shortest judged solution per problem", which changes nothing on a file with one row per problem. Text rows in all: median 222 reply tokens, at most 745. The other 221 problems have no text: they are learned only if the student comes to solve them itself.
- The twin, T:U6 on E2B: the same rounds, text and settings with the translator fed E2B's own states. T:U7 against T:U6 is the loop's form of cross against self (209i: 24 against 21 on held problems, p = 0.77).
**What differs from 177b's T:U6 on Qwen.** The student (E2B, dequantized QAT weights) and what feeds the translator (U7). The copied text is the judged one. For the 24 GB card, with no change to a loss: both models' per-layer embedding tables stay in CPU memory; E4B is loaded for each update only and its pass ends after its upper source layer (the states are the same to the last bit on the CPU test); an on-policy row scores its reply positions only (the positions its loss reads); generation batch 32 (the pilot: 64). Keep rows are limited to 512 reply tokens, which removes none (the longest of the 784 has 464, so the keep set is the uncapped one). A training step that runs out of memory is dropped and counted (none expected).
**What differs from 209n and 209r (the stream in online.py).** Rounds over the whole pool, not one pass of 598 items: 800 row visits per round. The corrector is not 209i's (fitted once on 602 reviewed rows with prose, questions and stories beside them): it is refitted every round on the round's wins alone, own and copied, and the teacher is model r-1 corrected, not untrained E2B corrected. The LoRA is rank 64 on the MLPs from the write layer up and is merged every round (the stream: rank 16, one adapter). No replay; the keep term is on untaught text only. The pool is 517 problems, 221 of them without any text.
**Reference levels (188 held problems; the stream's evaluation, batch 16).** Untrained E2B 57; E4B 74; the one-pass hybrid 80 and 69 (209n, seeds 1 and 2); copying E4B's verified text 76, 69, 75; the plain cross-model corrector 76, 75. Model 0 is measured again here (batch 32); if it is not within 3 of 57 the levels above are not comparable and only the paired tests are read. S:U1 on Qwen rose for two rounds and stopped (24, 37, 43, 40).
**Rules (T:U7 at round 3 unless said; paired by problem).** (1) "The loop teaches": more held problems gained than lost against model 0, sign test p < 0.05. (2) "It keeps improving": against its own round 1, more held problems gained than lost at round 3 with p < 0.1; the size is reported beside what two seeds of one recipe differ by (15 to 28 held problems in 209i). (3) "E4B's states add inside the loop": T:U7 ahead of T:U6 at round 3 on the held problems both model 0s failed, p < 0.1; otherwise "no measurable difference". (4) No stub: stub share at most 2% on the held problems in every round. (5) "It keeps what it had": at least 90% of the problems model 0 solved (held and train pool) still solved at round 3. Reported, no rule: unfinished answers per round, on-policy rows and problems with no own win per round, dropped steps, peak GPU memory.
**Predictions.** Rule 1 (75%). Held solved at round 3 at least E4B's 74 (40%); at least 80, the one-pass hybrid's best seed (10%). Rule 2 (20%); round 3 at least round 1 on held (55%). Rule 3 (8%); T:U7 at least T:U6 on held at round 3 (50%). Rule 4 (65%). Rule 5 (60%). The on-policy rows grow from round 1 to round 3 (85%); the train-pool problems with no own win fall in every round (80%).
**Limits.** One seed, one pair of models, three rounds on the same problems: the text is the same in every round, so after round 1 anything new must come from the student's own wins (the note of 15:26: the loop needs new problems each round and a stronger source for what E4B fails). 56 of the judge's picks are E2B's own verified answers, so on those problems the student copies its own model. The picks are one reviewer model's choice. The translator here is not the paper's corrector: a gain or a null in rule 3 speaks for this fit only. The update-phase memory was estimated, not measured (about 21 of 24.9 GB at the longest reply: the two models 13.1 GB with the tables and E4B's upper 14 layers in CPU memory, about 5 GB for the 262k-wide logits of a 768-token reply and their gradient); every phase logs its peak, the small pass on the real models first.
**Cost.** The 4090 only. Estimated from the pilot's timings and one E2B evaluation on Colab: about 8 hours for T:U7 and 6 for T:U6 at batch 32, give or take 40% (model 0's attempt, about 1.5 hours, is shared).
**Checks before queuing (CPU, tiny random Gemma 4 models with the real tokenizer, the real pools, tests in the sandbox, the real row files).** (1) With every new option off, the new script gives the old one's results on arm T:U6: the result files agree in all 153 numbers with no tolerance, the wins, answers and samples files are the same and both rounds' adapters are the same byte for byte. (2) RSI_LEAN and RSI_PLE_CPU change no number: the same comparison against the plain run, adapters again identical; on one row the reply positions of the full-span logits equal the reply-only logits exactly. (3) The stronger model's states with the early exit equal those of the whole pass exactly. (4) T:U7 runs end to end in small mode with all options (2 rounds; per round 15 on-policy rows, 4 text rows, 4 keep rows; E4B loaded and released each round; both merges sound), its twin T:U6 reuses model 0 in the same folder, and the summary compares the two. (5) A simulated out-of-memory error in the translator fit and one in the LoRA update each drop one step and the run goes on. (6) On the full pool the row files load as counted above (0 rows rejected). Not tested: anything on the GPU (the real models' loading with the tables in CPU memory, the peak memory, the time); the launcher's small pass on the real models comes first and stops the arm, not the queue, if it fails. The models' sha256 on the 4090 match the Colab sessions' (E4B 14b754d4..., E2B fff95a79...). Code: rsi.py md5 d4a1945a... in ~/rsi4090/bench3 beside the pilot's own xbridge, xcode and xlearn. 
**Review (a separate agent; reading, helper tests on fake data, a dry run of the launcher in a fake home).** rsi.py: no defect; with the options unset and another rule nothing changes; held problems' text cannot enter training; a judged row with another prompt or without its one closing fence is refused; E4B reads the same token ids in eval mode without gradients and is released before the merge; the LoRA's target is computed with the adapter off; a dropped step takes no optimizer step and is counted. The launcher: four faults, fixed before queuing and checked in the dry run. (1) The job started at its end inherited this launcher's settings (batch 32 would have made 177b recompute the pilot's model 0, so its arms would no longer be paired with the pilot's): it now runs in the home folder with those settings unset. (2) The check for a finished arm read a log named without the seed: a marker in the arm's own folder now decides. (3) The code and inputs were checked only after the wait and the checksum of rsi.py was optional: both are checked at the start and again after the wait, and the checksum is required. (4) The small pass ran with the out-of-memory skip on, so a row that does not fit could not fail it: the skip is off in the small pass and a dropped step there stops the arm. Left as limits: dropped steps, if any, may fall more on T:U7 than on its twin (E4B takes memory during the update); the 56 judged picks written by E2B are counted under "stronger" in the bookkeeping. Launcher md5 91d2199b...
**Order on the 4090 (changed from the note of 16:35).** The pilot ends (about 19:30), then this loop (T:U7, then its twin), then 177b's four arms as registered. 177b's waiting launcher is stopped and started again by this loop's launcher when it ends; nothing in 177b changes.

## Amendment 209r, seeds 2 and 3 (00:09, Nov 12; registered before the runs)
User, 00:05: "is the judged hybrid also runnung?" (the loop of 177c copies judged text; of the one-pass form only seed 1 had run, a second seed needing about 29 CU with 28.2 left); 00:08: "i added 100 more cu". Read as the word for the missing seeds: seeds 2 and 3 of 209r (sessions xqzR2, xqzR3), so that the judged hybrid has the plain hybrid's three seeds. Everything is 209r's (start_r.sh: code15, the corrector 7571b2b8..., the judged rows 20a20378..., the same md5s as seed 1); only ON_SEED differs.
**Reference (seeds 1, 2, 3).** Plain hybrid (209n): held solved 80, 69, 77; fixed 99, 100, 110 of 151; unfinished 116, 113, 75 of 487; placeholders 0, 5, 0. Judged hybrid, seed 1: held 75, fixed 109, unfinished 71, no placeholder, 54 held answers finished and wrong (plain hybrid 25).
**Rules.** (1) "The judged text leaves fewer answers unfinished than the plain hybrid": fewer in each of the three seed pairs and, pooled over the three seeds by answer (unfinished in one run only), an exact sign test with p < 0.05; otherwise "not shown". (2) On held problems the judged hybrid is "level with the plain hybrid" unless the pooled paired test over three seeds gives p < 0.05; "ahead" also needs more held problems solved in every seed pair. (3) 209r's rule as written, per new seed: at most 105 unfinished, at least 70 held, at most 2 placeholders.
**Predictions.** At most 105 unfinished at seed 2 (70%) and at seed 3 (70%); fewer unfinished than the plain hybrid at seed 2 (75%) and at seed 3 (40%; the plain hybrid's 75 is its lowest); rule 1 met (35%). At least 70 held at seed 2 (65%) and at seed 3 (65%); three-seed total of held problems at least the plain hybrid's 226 (30%); the pooled held test does not separate the two (85%). No placeholder in either seed (60%). At least 100 of 151 fixed in both (60%). More held answers finished and wrong than the plain hybrid at the same seed, in both (70%).
**Cost.** Two G4 sessions of about 3 hours, about 29 CU each (the user's 100 CU added to 28.2).
**Paper.** If both end before the submission in the morning: the 209r row of Table D9, the variant's sentences in the hybrid paragraph and the scorecard row get three seeds; the body changes only if rule 2 reads "ahead" or rule 1 is met (one clause, one cut).

"9% of losses were answers cut at the cap" is 9% that pass with the cap doubled (59 of 665; 176 were cut); "online, two seeds" is 2-3; seeds of one recipe differ by up to 13 held problems (79 and 66), not 12; "solved no more than the self-corrector's" is "did not separate from" (151 against 148; 24 vs 21, p = 0.77); 4.1's 200 failed training problems are Qwen's (E2B: 151); the release holds over 100,000 graded answers (104,626 lines in 215 files), not 77,000.

- T:U6 round 3 against model 0: held 25 gained and 7 lost (p = 0.0021); train pool 134 of 374 fixed, 3 of 143 lost; 182 of the 192 problems model 0 solved are kept (95%). Round against round on held: round 2 against round 1 14 against 6 (p = 0.12); round 3 against round 2 9 against 13; round 3 against round 1 14 against 10 (p = 0.54). No stub on held problems in any round.
- T:U6's rows in rounds 1, 2, 3: on-policy 678, 1,024, 1,133; text rows 90, 30, 13 (the judge's pick 71, 19, 8); problems with no own win 304, 228, 203, of those with no text either 214, 198, 190. Held answers cut at the token limit, models 0 to 3: 108, 96, 69, 68. No step dropped; the LoRA update peaks at 19.0 GB. Rounds of 30, 52 and 52 minutes. Off-task, model 3 against model 0: untaught prose 4.369 against 4.473 (-0.104 [-0.124, -0.083]); fresh questions p(true) 0.560 against 0.582 (-0.023 [-0.031, -0.014]); held agent rows 0.683 against 0.685.
- Rule 3 ("E4B's states add inside the loop": T:U7 ahead of T:U6 at round 3 on the 139 held problems model 0 failed, p < 0.1): not met, 8 against 7 (26 and 25 of the 139). "No measurable difference." On all held problems at round 3, 12 against 9 (70 and 67; p = 0.66); pooled over the three rounds 38 against 38; at round 2 the twin led, 16 against 11 (71 and 66; p = 0.44).
- On the train pool, not registered: round 1 28 against 43 (p = 0.096, the twin ahead), round 2 31 against 40, round 3 35 against 24 (p = 0.19; on the 374 problems model 0 failed 32 against 19, p = 0.092). No consistent side.
- The arms differ by more than the translator's source: T:U7 dropped 50 of its 1,200 update steps for memory (18, 18, 14), T:U6 none.
- Predictions, the last two: rule 3 (8%): failed. T:U7 at least T:U6 on held at round 3 (50%): held (70, 67). In all 7 of the 11 hold.
- Reading. In the loop, as in the one-pass runs (209i: 24 against 21) and on the agent stream, the corrector's form carries the gain and E4B's states add nothing that the held problems show. Both arms gain most in round 1 (the twin also in round 2) and then move inside the noise (65, 66, 70 and 63, 71, 67), while the train pool keeps rising in every round (to 285 and 274 of 517): after the copied text is used up (90 text rows, then about 30, then 13 to 17) the loop consolidates the problems it trains on. Model 0 here solves 49 held problems against Colab's 57, so these levels are not set beside the one-pass runs. One seed. Result files: kaggle_g4agent/runs/rsi4090c/g1.

- Teaching. Seed 2: 771 of 1,494 samples pass; 254 of 747 learned items had no passing sample; 249 learned as text (224 the judged solution, 25 the row's own text). Seed 3: 771 of 1,494; 254 of 747; 249 (226 and 23). The three totals are equal in the two seeds by chance: the logs differ block by block (the second block of 60 items: 66 against 53 samples passing), the seed offsets are 2000 and 3000 and the answers differ. Prose cross-entropy 4.492 and 4.471 (E2B alone 4.473), drift 0.012 and 0.015, agent gate net 0 (4 fixed, 4 broken) and -5 (5 and 10).
- Rule 1 ("the judged text leaves fewer answers unfinished than the plain hybrid"): met. 71, 72, 59 against 116, 113, 75; by answer, unfinished in one run only: 15 against 60 (seed 1), 15 against 56 (seed 2), 23 against 39 (seed 3; p = 0.056 alone); pooled 53 against 155, p < 0.0001 (8.6e-13).
- Rule 2: "level with the plain hybrid". Held problems by problem 11 against 16, 14 against 13, 12 against 18; pooled 37 against 47, p = 0.33; totals 216 against 226; more held in one of the three seed pairs (70 against 69). Row problems 36 against 27, 31 against 36, 30 against 41; pooled 97 against 104, p = 0.67.
- Rule 3 (209r's rule per new seed: at most 105 unfinished, at least 70 held, at most 2 stubs): met at seed 2 (72, 70, 1); not met at seed 3 (59, 71, 3 stubs, all three on held problems).
- Predictions: at most 105 unfinished at seed 2 (70%): held; at seed 3 (70%): held; fewer than the plain hybrid at seed 2 (75%): held; at seed 3 (40%): held; rule 1 (35%): held; at least 70 held at seed 2 (65%): held, 70; at seed 3 (65%): held, 71; three-seed total at least 226 (30%): failed, 216; the pooled held test does not separate (85%): held; no stub in either seed (60%): failed, 1 and 3; at least 100 of 151 fixed in both (60%): held, 101 and 106; more held answers finished and wrong than the plain hybrid at the same seed in both (70%): held, 58 against 32 and 65 against 52. 10 of 12.
- What takes the place of the unfinished answers. Of the 155 answers unfinished for the plain hybrid and not for the judged one, the judged student's are 56 passing, 94 wrong, 2 that do not parse and 3 stubs (on held problems, 92: 13 passing, 74 wrong, 2 and 3). Of the 53 unfinished only for the judged one, the plain hybrid's are 27 passing, 23 wrong and 3 stubs.
- Against the other teachers, pooled by problem: E4B's distribution on passing answers, held 43 against 39 (p = 0.74), row problems 145 against 91 (p = 0.0005); E4B's verified text, 32 against 36 (p = 0.72) and 89 against 96 (p = 0.66); the plain cross-model corrector (two seeds), 19 against 25 (p = 0.45) and 96 against 58 (p = 0.003); untrained E2B, 66 against 21.
- Reading. Copying the short judged solution makes the student finish its answers at all three seeds, and its answers are the shortest; that is the registered claim and it holds. It does not make the student solve more: most of what now finishes on held problems is wrong, the held total is 10 below the plain hybrid's (inside the seed noise, p = 0.33) and the training problems are level. User, 19:45, Nov 11: "i think if we do the hybrid with a judge that will be even better": better in the form of the answers, not in problems solved, on these three seeds.
- Paper. As registered (rule 1 met: one clause, one cut): 4.5's online paragraph gains "A shorter reviewer-picked solution instead left fewer answers unfinished (59-72 against 75-116) without solving more." and the hybrid's sentence is shortened ("by copying E4B's verified text where no answer passes"); cut: "(Appendix D)" after the fit sentence, "by problem" before 43 vs 84, "own" and "from" in the first sentence. Body 3,000 words. Table D9's row carries three seeds (caption: seed 3 for four rows), the variant's sentences in the hybrid paragraph are rewritten for three seeds, the scorecard has a row for this registration (47 rows; SCORECARD.md synced).
